## 2026-10-06 · Grind ollie intent and deliberate rail catches

- Brief: distinguish an ollie/spin back onto the rail from a side exit or rail-to-rail transfer by direction timing. Follow-up: require another Grind press for the landing; missing it must bail.
- Elapsed prompt-to-playable: approximately **14 minutes** through native keyboard lite/full gameplay verification; isolated release and deployment verification followed.
- Sample the stick before Jump release. A neutral pop keeps the rail tangent and forward speed; subsequent left/right rotates without steering. Pre-held lateral direction launches at up to **4.8 m/s**, with a **0.3** intent deadzone, analog strength and **80–100%** charge scaling. Measured same-height displacement is **2.11/3.28 m** for tap/full campaign pops and **2.24/3.52 m** in parks. The launch direction does not also rotate the rider; release/repress or change direction to spin. Carry side momentum through grabs, rail catches and ground roll-outs. Retire R2/grab trajectory overrides; preserve authored vertical pop, gravity, ordinary board/on-foot air controls and balance difficulty.
- A fresh airborne Grind edge arms the next catch, including an early press held through cooldown. The original held button does not count on either rail. Same-rail catches require descent through the rail contact envelope; unarmed landings use the existing rail bail. Endpoint run-offs retain their anti-recatch latch, and catches/landings/respawns clear the ollie state.
- Validation: **68 scenarios / 4,516 actual controller frames**; neutral 180s, both rail directions, angled/sloping screen frames, analog/noise, tap/full charge, same-frame versus pre-held input, side exits, adjacent rails, fresh/stale/released Grind, modifier independence, roll-out, respawn and endpoints. Existing flip-chord, bare-spin scoring, balance re-entry and all **51 coping drop-in cases** pass. **24 native keyboard cases** in real Chrome cover campaign/park and lite/full rendering with no console errors. Production type-check and Vite bundle pass on an isolated latest-main checkout. No level data changes or full-suite run.
- Reproducible checks: `node tools/test-grind-ollie-intent.mjs`, `node tools/test-rail-air-transfer.mjs --controls-only`, and `node tools/grind-ollie-browser.mjs <base-url>`. The legacy rail-air test's cross-grind torso-facing pose assertion already failed on the unchanged pre-task baseline; its optional pose section remains intact, with controls isolated for this gameplay regression. Preserve all unrelated workspace edits when publishing.

- Keep the slide attack's authored distance. On ice, scale its launch deceleration by the contacted surface's grip and transfer the remaining world-space velocity to ordinary on-foot ice movement. Finish the fractional final step as a coast. Dry ground retains the exact stop, and crossing from ice onto dry ground brakes across the remaining attack distance.
- Keep this carry on foot until an explicit board commitment. A held Circle cannot replace the moving coast with a planted crawl, and the dry recovery pause cannot erase an ice exit. Retire carry ownership at low speed, on action changes and on reset; retain normal slide-jump grace and action controls. No tuning-default or level-data changes.
- Focused checks cover **16** held/released slides across eight directions, uninterrupted handoff, normal ice drag, no automatic crawl/board, dry/ice boundaries, slide-jump grace, deliberate board commitment and respawn. With default ice grip, a 26 m/s slide exits at **24.390 m/s** and coasts approximately **20.097 m** over the next 61 fixed steps. Dry slides retain exactly **5 m** of travel and their get-up recovery. Existing slide-jump, crouch/jump/slam, authored ice physics and ice-walk animation regressions pass, as do production type-check/build. No full suite.
- Real Chrome tests released and held Circle on Sky Bridge in lite and full rendering. After standing out of the slide, speed remains **21.797 m/s**, with no recovery lock, forced crawl, board mount or console errors. A normal on-foot jump carries to the next supported falling deck beyond the sanctuary, with no deaths. Evidence: `tools/test-ice-slide-carry.mjs`, `tools/test-ice-slide-browser.mjs`, `docs/performance/ice-slide-*.json` and `docs/art-reviews/ice-slide-coast.jpg`.
- Replay limitation: the supplied 4,356-frame `replay-SkyBridge-2026-10-08T10-38-29.json` reproduces five Circle slides but no ice contacts on the current build. Use the direct current-level and controller cases above as the evidence for this fix, rather than claiming that older take faithfully reproduced its ice section.


## 2026-10-09 — Shared hard scenery contacts (Custard Creek replay)

- Brief: the recorded spillway crash exposed a rendered stone wall authored as `solid:false`. Make visible hard structures collide throughout the game, with outward impacts and ragdolls, including fallen bodies, loose parts and thrown boards.
- Elapsed prompt-to-first browser-verified playable: **173 minutes** (goal started 2026-10-08 08:35:55 UTC; first recorded approach proof 11:28:29 UTC). The complete global validation continued after that first playable result.
- Add a scene-owned spatial index and shared local geometry BVHs. Continuous capsule sweeps use exact transformed triangles on both sides, preserve openings, and cover moving/rotating geometry. Bind loaded/instanced models independently of render visibility and retire their contacts with their scene ownership. Existing native contact geometry wins over legacy enclosing AABBs. Crumble/phase deactivation remains authored.
- Keep scenery's hard/soft identity through batching, editor controls and capture. Native Beachside cliffs, timber posts, masonry, city buildings, castle scenery and rooted trunks now participate; foliage, water, painted lines, mattes and canvas stay permeable. Standing on hard scenery uses the same measured geometry. Native timber decks retain their continuous seam-free ride surface. Map/hub movement retains its own contact rules.
- Fit the standing collision body to character stature and the fallen body to its lowered pose. Reflect strong impacts along the contact normal through the existing bail system. Preserve the established step-up window, floor/pipe/loop ownership and ledge traversal. Constrain late rail/crate reactions too: the old rail-trip reposition could place a ragdoll behind a stone wall after its first collision pass.
- Share the scenery index with floor rays while retaining each actual support mesh's identity and immediate native support removal. In the same 1280×720 Chrome review, median fixed-step CPU time in Jungle fell from **4.0 to 3.1 ms**, and Ghost Train from **5.9 to 4.7 ms**. A 5,000-surface stress case visits **one candidate / 12 triangles** and averages about **0.02 ms** per nearby sweep. These are host samples, not a universal FPS claim.
- Validation: shared geometry/policy tests, finite angled-ray hit parity, moving/retired support, thin-wall sweeps, ragdoll/corpse/head/board rebound, the **112-probe Jungle enclosure** sweep, **12 moving-carriage vaults**, loop completion/retry, **316 scenery capture checks**, native Beachside/boardwalk checks, map movement, ice-slide carry and production build. The continuous Custard chapter completes in **39.43 s**, with **42 crates**, no bails and no deaths. No full `check:all` run.
- Browser evidence: sampled hard faces in Custard Creek, Treehouse Trail (including the newly merged opening), Beachside, Nightworks, Ghost Train and Jungle all collide; consoles are clean. The supplied take's isolated final approach hits **Custard spillway outer stone mass at frame 6348**, at **21.577 m/s** into the face, rebounds outward at **6.905 m/s**, and ragdolls. With new collision active from frame zero, the old inputs take a different route and reach game over at frame 3462; do not present that altered route as an exact reproduction of the final approach. Evidence and reproduction commands: `docs/performance/world-solid-contacts.json`, `tools/solid-wall-replay-browser.mjs`, and `tools/solid-world-browser.mjs`.
- Existing unrelated failures were reproduced on untouched `751d93e`: the Sky Bridge copy/fog assertion in editor round-trip, the fixed-frame legacy Jungle ledge replay, and the cross-grind torso-facing assertion in rail-air transfer. Their implementations are outside this change.
- Deployment crate checks: isolate the attack-forgiveness fixture from the finish pad and seed its swept origin when placing the body directly. The unchanged spin/stomp/hazard assertions pass, as do crate-jump parity and typed-crate checks.

Remove a redundant non-indexed geometry conversion warning. No movement, visual-quality or geometry changes in this patch.

Treehouse's complete loading transition falls **6,302 → 5,555 ms** (**12% less**). Nightworks's complete transition falls **6,440 → 4,838 ms**. These are this host's measurements at the unchanged 720p/1× full-render preset, not a phone FPS claim.
- Check all **55 levels** in lite and full baseline passes, and repeat all **55** against the final combined production build. Verify all **145 checkpoint** landings, supported spawns, correct native directional input, both competition starts, asset readiness and clean error consoles. A separate full-render lifecycle pass checks all **55** for fall recovery, applicable checkpoints and ordinary finish gates; repeat ten representative flows after integrating current main. Gate placement and checkpoint warps are explicitly not full-course traversal claims.
- Exact collision regression matches **634 contacts, 2,182 misses and 96 moving/phase sweeps** without tolerance. Loading scheduling/state/error regressions, the exact focused level checks and production type-check/build pass; the integrated world-surface binding test passes **47 checks**. Add the two focused loading/collision tests to normal deployment CI. No full-suite run.
- Preserve two pre-existing test failures: the old Nightworks test expects a zero-height spawn beneath the raised arrival pad; the After Hours pilot misses its second counterweight at tick **2,096**, with an identical complete failure state before/after this optimization. Document the initial audit's incorrect Up-input probes for horizontal rooms; the corrected production input checks pass all rooms.
- The full goal remains active: complete intended traversal coverage, investigation of the After Hours pilot mismatch, first-use effect/phase-rock/fruit/HUD GPU work, repeated-return residency and touch/recovery checks remain. Per-level coverage and limitations: **docs/GAMEPLAY_PERFORMANCE_AUDIT.md**. ## 2026-10-18 · Modular golf carts and silly arcade driving

- Brief: an original Crash 4-inspired, low-poly cart with easy independent recolouring, interchangeable drivers, five simultaneous instances, collision and visible damage. Spinning and hard player contacts affect carts through bounded impulses and the existing knockdown. Preserve player movement tuning and existing level data.
- Use 120 Hz fixed arcade physics, two-circle chassis contacts, steering/spinning wheels, independent suspension, local dents and a bounded chip pool. Shift gives a **1.22 m standing hop**; ramp crests preserve and amplify launch (**1.89 m air** in the focused probe). Normal hops do not accumulate damage. Hard side impacts or fast badly banked landings can roll the entire cart, with a finite self-righting settle. Normal grounded cornering does not randomly flip.
- Focused validation passes exact 30/60/144 Hz trajectory agreement, braking/reverse, head-on/barrel/overlap contacts, unsafe respawn, bounded catch-up, hop/landing/roll recovery, five AI carts for one minute, all five course patrols, palette isolation, driver swaps, triangle budgets and shared-resource disposal. Final browser review has no console errors/warnings. The supplied drivers are rigid articulated segments; physics is arcade planar collision with vertical flight and controlled roll, not arbitrary convex rigid-body simulation.

- Brief: improve the frog hop. Separate the existing .45-second crouch into impact absorption, a finite rebound/settle, and a fresh load. Keep the existing trajectory, speed, collision boxes, combat windows, and source GLB.
- Use the shared editable species/character elasticity strengths for segment compression and stretch. Ease planted-foot correction out over the first .085 seconds of flight and introduce limb deformation with that release, preserving the complete supported pose at the takeoff boundary. Keep all four grounded sole matrices fixed; never scale the actor or complete skeleton. Reviewed front, quarter, and side playback and the actual Jungle encounter.
- `tools/test-frog-hop.mjs` samples every vertex of the shipping skin at 240 Hz, including state boundaries, supported soles, floor clearance, reset/repeated samples, root ownership, distinct silhouettes, and finite landing recovery. It fails on unmodified published code (17.9 cm skin discontinuity) and passes after the change (largest adjacent sample displacement 5.7 cm, no floor penetration). Measured silhouette heights: rest 2.189 m, loaded 1.841 m, kick 2.528 m, tuck 2.068 m. Update the reviewed vertical envelope to 2.6 m; retain the original width/depth and floor limits.
- Also cover the real touchdown frame, which still reports the last airborne horizontal displacement. Suppress the frog’s fallback walk, walking breath/glance changes, and alternating contacts on that frame. The regression reproduces a 56.8 cm skin snap and 26.1 cm floor penetration before the fix, then verifies identical landing poses with zero or carried speed, including active glances and alternate gait phases.
- Validation: enemy asset, runtime, gameplay, and polish checks; the new real-skin regression; production TypeScript/Vite builds. Browser review includes lite/full rendering, supported placement, real spin collision, checkpoint activation, pit death/automatic respawn, and finish handling, with console checks. No level-data, movement-tuning, or unrelated workspace changes are included in the release; no full-suite run.
